Chapter 1 : The Beginning of Brahma’s Creation

Salutations to Shri Ganesha.

Shaunaka and the other sages said: O Suta, you are a great soul; you have fulfilled our purpose by narrating the incomparable and magnificent character of the Lord with the curved trunk. You are blessed and possess great wisdom, and you are especially omniscient. We ask you to explain this to us truthfully. We never tire of hearing these stories, as they are like drinking nectar. Therefore, please narrate the character of the One-Tusked Lord now. In which Brahman does this One-Tusked Elephant-faced Lord reside? How many are His incarnations, and what are His deeds? Please tell us definitively.

Being thus requested, Suta, bowing with humility and moved by their devotion, spoke to the great sages.

Suta said: O great sages, listen to what the great sage Mudgala said when he was asked the same questions by Daksha.

Daksha said: O best of sages, I possess great merit from the past, which is why I have been blessed with the vision of an omniscient being like you. I have heard the character of the Curved-trunk Lord from your lotus-like mouth, and my heart has found great peace. The sorrow I felt from the destruction of my sacrifice has now vanished, O Lord. By the grace of Dhundhi (Ganesha), I am filled with joy. Please tell me specifically about the One-Tusked Lord—what are His deeds, how did He originate, what is His vehicle, and what is the nature of His Brahman? O ocean of compassion, please tell me all of this.

Suta said: Being thus asked, the glorious Mudgala, knowing Daksha’s deep devotion to Ganesha, the giver of all success, spoke to the Prajapati.

Mudgala said: Listen, O Daksha, to what you have asked. The greatness of the One-Tusked Lord destroys all sins and grants both worldly enjoyment and liberation. You are blessed, O great soul, for you have a keen interest in hearing the character of the Elephant-faced God in all its aspects. I shall narrate to you the ancient history, including the dialogue between Gritsamada and the great-souled Prahlada.

Hearing the words of Mudgala, Daksha felt joyful and, bowing humbly, spoke to the great sage.

Daksha said: O Lord, please begin with the creation of Brahma and describe the path of creation, how the three worlds were created by Brahma, the source of the universe. In that context, the greatness of Prahlada will naturally arise; please describe the dialogue with Gritsamada as it occurred.

Suta said: Hearing the words of Daksha, Mudgala spoke respectfully about the path of creation and the events of the past, which had been obscured by a curse.

Mudgala said: Listen, O wise Daksha. I shall tell you how Brahma, the grandfather of the worlds, created the diverse universe. By the boon of Ganesha, Brahma resolved to create. Taking refuge in Yoga-Maya, he entered into Janardana (Vishnu). After dwelling there for a year, he emerged from the lotus of the navel. By the boon of Vishnu, Brahma became His son. Sitting on that lotus, he meditated upon Heramba (Ganesha) within his heart. Then, a great deal of water flowed from the body of Brahma. As he looked, the water covered all ten directions. He became submerged in that water and wandered with a confused mind. He remembered the Lord of the Ganas: "O Lord, protect me from this terror! O Dhundhi, I am sinking in this water; O Curved-trunk Lord, salutations to You!"

By merely remembering Him, he saw a magnificent banyan tree. Seeing Ganesha sleeping on a leaf there, he was filled with joy. He saw the three-eyed, four-armed Lord, adorned with a trunk, with a large belly, the serpent as His navel, the moon on His forehead, and wearing a crown. He was complete in all limbs, adorned with various ornaments, and covered in garments, appearing as a child the size of a thumb. Seeing Ganesha in this form, Brahma was astonished, wondering how this banyan tree remained in the midst of the great water. Brahma approached the Lord of the Ganas, who then sprinkled water on Brahma with His trunk. Delighted, Brahma praised the Elephant-faced Lord with folded hands and a heart softened by devotion.

Brahma said: Salutations to You, O Lord of the Ganas, who plays in the waters of dissolution. Salutations again and again to Heramba, who rests on a banyan leaf. Salutations to the four-armed One, whose navel is the serpent. Salutations to the Elephant-faced, the Lord of all, the Large-bellied One. Salutations to the One-Tusked Lord, adorned with various beauties, who assumes the form of a thumb-sized being. Salutations to Ganesha, who is accompanied by Siddhi and Buddhi, who protects His devotees, and who possesses infinite glory. Salutations to the One who is both Nirguna (without attributes) and Saguna (with attributes), the foundation of all qualities. Salutations to the One who performs divine plays through various forms. Salutations to the beginningless Child-form, the creator, sustainer, and destroyer of the universe, the master of Maya, the giver of great delusion. O Elephant-faced Lord, remove the great obstacles of the creator. I have been forcibly submerged in this terrible water. I have taken refuge in You; protect me, O lover of devotees. I am Your servant, O Lord of gods; have mercy, O ocean of compassion.

After praising Him, Brahma saw in the Lord’s trunk the entire universe, moving and stationary, along with its presiding deities. Then, carried by a divine breeze, Brahma entered the Lord’s belly, where he saw the universe again, filled with various wonders. Seeing the belly of the Lord containing many such universes, Brahma exited through a pore of His skin as before. He saw the child-form Ganesha and was filled with wonder. The Lord then spoke to Brahma, His great devotee.

Shri Ganesha said: This hymn composed by you will be pleasing to Me. It will grant all desires and bestow both worldly enjoyment and liberation. Whoever reads or hears it will be dear to Me, and whatever they contemplate in their heart, I will surely grant. O Grandfather, you emerged from the navel of Vishnu by His boon, but you forgot to remember and worship Me at the beginning. That is why you were beset by obstacles and reborn. Now, having remembered Me and seen Me, you are free from obstacles. Henceforth, O wise One, you have a task to perform. By remembering and bowing to Me, you will attain success. Just as you have seen the moving and stationary world in My belly today, create that same universe now by My command.

Having said this, Ganesha, in His child form, immediately disappeared. Brahma sat on the lotus and became as he was before. Then, the lotus-seated Brahma chanted the Ganesha mantra, worshipped Him according to the scriptures, and became absorbed in meditation. The Elephant-faced Lord appeared within his heart. Brahma bowed to Him and performed mental worship. At the time of the final offering, Siddhi and Buddhi, the two supreme energies, appeared in his heart, and he offered them to the Remover of Obstacles. Having accepted the worship performed according to the scriptures, Ganesha, the Lord of all, immediately disappeared. With Ganesha, the embodiment of Brahman, residing in his heart, Brahma bowed to Him and resolved to begin the creation.

Thus ends the first chapter of the second section of the glorious Mudgala Purana, which deals with the character of the One-Tusked Lord and the beginning of Brahma’s creation.

Chapter 2 : The Revocation of Narada's Curse

Salutations to Shri Ganesha.

Mudgala said: Brahma, having meditated upon Ganesha in his mind, prepared to create the universe. First, from the body of that Creator, Avidya (Ignorance) emerged. She, the daughter of Prajapati, manifested in five forms. Seeing her, Brahma was astonished and felt sorrow in his heart. This Avidya, characterized by delusion and possessing infinite power, appeared in red, black, and white colors, filled with various wonders. She was the mother of three types of beings and the enchantress of the three worlds. The world, deluded by her, became filled with confusion.

O Daksha, hear of these five great wonders that exist in the world: Tamas (darkness), Moha (attachment), Mahamoha (great attachment), Tamisra (darkness/anger), and Andhatamisra (blind darkness). Tamas is the form of ignorance, by which the world is covered; it became deluded and obsessed with the body and self-interest. Moha is that which creates various actions driven by desire; through it, the world performs actions that bring about the fruits of bodily experience. Mahamoha is that which is connected to various sinful actions; through it, a man commits sins for the sake of bodily pleasure. Tamisra creates the fruits of disease, poverty, and the like; by it, the world became afflicted with physical and mental suffering. From Andhatamisra, the hells of torment arise; through it, the world suffered the consequences of its own actions.

This Avidya, remembering Ganesha, became self-centered. Then Brahma, condemning her, decided to create again. From his body, four sons were born: the great yogi Sanaka, and likewise Sanandana, Sanatana, and the fourth, Sanatkumara. In the form of their mother, Vidya (Knowledge) appeared as the essence of wisdom. Seeing her, who was in the form of pure consciousness, formless, and the embodiment of the supreme Self, Brahma, the grandfather of all, was filled with joy. Sanaka appeared as the bearer of natural knowledge; Sanandana, O Daksha, was a powerful yogi; Sanatana was known as a yogi of Jnana-yoga (the path of knowledge); and Sanatkumara appeared as the embodiment of Karma-yoga (the path of action).

Seeing them, the Lord of the world was filled with joy. Remembering Ganesha, he created the universe, and they did the same. Those powerful beings created various paths of yoga. Seeing this, Brahma was not pleased and said to them again, "Enough of this yoga-filled creation; do not create." Saying "So be it," those great beings stood still.

Brahma then became angry upon seeing the creation of knowledge. From his forehead, Shambhu (Shiva) was born as his son. Brahma praised the Lord, who was radiant with brilliance, five-faced, three-eyed, ten-armed, and adorned with the crescent moon. He was half-man, half-woman, wise, marked with the trident and other symbols, matted-haired, large-bodied, and wearing tiger skin. Upon being born, Shankara suddenly wept. Brahma consoled him, asking, "Why do you weep, O rider of the bull?" The child said to Brahma, "Father, O Lord, give me a place, names, and tell me my duties in detail."

The Creator replied to him, "Because you wept, you shall be called Rudra. You are Shiva, the foundation of all happiness; you have been born as Maheshwara. By the power of my boon, you are now the Lord of all. You have come as my son in the form of Shankara. Earth, water, fire, air, space, the moon, the sun, and the initiated one—these eight are your places, O Shankara. You are Sharva, the bearer of eight forms, the giver of fruits to all. Let your names be eleven, O Lord of gods. Create the universe, O Mahadeva, by meditating upon the God with the elephant face." Saying "So be it," Shiva began creation after meditating upon Ganapati.

Shambhu created various Rudras of great brilliance, infinite and of diverse forms, possessing the nature of immortality. Seeing them, Brahma said, "O Lord of the world, do not create these. O Shambhu, create beings who are subject to birth and death." Then Shiva said to the Creator, "I will not create those subject to birth and death," and saying this, he remained silent. Because of this, he was called Sthanu (the motionless one). Shiva himself remained as a celibate, worshipping Ganapati.

Then Brahma created the Vedas, accompanied by the scriptures and Puranas, from his mouths. He said to them, with folded hands, "Create various types of beings." Saying "So be it," they worshipped Ganapati with penance and created beings through his boons. They created various actions with great devotion. Seeing this, the Creator was filled with joy.

Then Brahma began a sacrifice and meditated upon Vishnu. For the sake of a son, that great Lord also became his son. From the sacrifice, the four-armed Lord, the embodiment of sacrifice, Janardana (Vishnu), appeared. He said with folded hands, "Give me your command, Father." Then Brahma said to him, "Create the universe, O Ganesha," and remembering him, he did so. Hari created various actions born of sacrifice, which were full of diverse fruits, like the wish-fulfilling tree. Seeing this, Brahma was delighted and then created again. From his body, ten great sons were born: Marichi, Bhrigu, Atri, Pulastya, Pulaha, Kratu, Daksha, Angira, Vashistha, and Narada.

Brahma himself told them, "Create various beings." Saying "Om," they all went to the best forest for penance. Then the sage Narada said to his father, "I will not create, O Lord of gods; I will worship the Lord of the Ganas. All this is illusory, false, and full of delusion. He who knows this and still labors for it is a fool, there is no doubt." Then he told him again, "Create various beings; in the end of your life, worship the Lord Ganesha, my son." He replied, "Why do you instruct me, O Prajapati? Who, being deluded, creates that which is false in the beginning?"

Then Brahma cursed him, "Fall into the womb of a Shudra. You shall become a Gandharva and be the enjoyer of many women." Then Narada, filled with anger, cursed his father, "You shall be unworthy of worship because you have cursed me in vain." Then Narada went and performed penance. He worshipped Ganesha with the single-syllable mantra. After a hundred divine years, Ganapati himself appeared to him and said, "Choose a boon, O great one; I will grant your heart's desire."

Then he praised the Lord Ganesha, the giver of all success, and chose this boon: "Revoke my curse." Then the Lord of the Ganas, who is fond of his devotees, said to him, "How can I make the father's curse false? I have given a boon to Brahma, and he is truthful. Therefore, hear my words, which are always beneficial. You will become a Gandharva; there, I will give you knowledge. Even though you are always with women, Maya will not bind you. Again, you will be born in the womb of a Shudra; there is no doubt. When you meet the ascetics there, you will gain knowledge from them and go to the forests. There, you will be attached to the path of the Vaishnavas. After the fall of that body, you will again be the son of Brahma, Narada; there is no doubt. There, you will worship Shambhu. Knowing me through the mouth of Narayana, O sage, you will become my devotee, a master of yoga, and a Gana-pati; there is no doubt."

Having said this, the Lord of the Ganas disappeared right there. Narada, having bowed to him and endured the curse, went to heaven. He was born again as Narada, devoted to Shiva, and having been instructed by Narayana, he became a Gana-pati.

Thus ends the second chapter of the second section of the Mudgala Purana, titled "The Revocation of Narada's Curse."

Chapter 3 : The Description of Narada’s Devotion

Daksha said: O best of sages, how was Narada instructed by Narayana? Please tell me this, O king of yogis, for I am never satisfied with the nectar of these stories.

Mudgala said: Once, while wandering, Narada went to see Shankara (Shiva). There, he saw Shiva worshipping Lord Ganesha. Shiva was worshipping an idol of Chintamani made of jewels, which had the face of an elephant with a trunk. Seeing Shiva engaged in such a disciplined ritual, the sage Narada was astonished. After bowing to him, he respectfully went to Vaikuntha to see Lord Vishnu.

Narada, filled with great devotion and humility, bowed to the mighty Vishnu and said: Salutations to you, O Narayana. You are the Lord of the worlds; please remove my doubt. I went to Kailasa to see Shiva, and there, O Janardana, I saw him worshipping an idol of Ganesha with great devotion, his body covered in goosebumps. Seeing Shiva in meditation, with tears of love flowing from his eyes, I remained silent. After bowing to him, I have come to you. You, too, chant the name and mantra of the Lord of the Ganas. It is said that there is nothing beyond Shiva, who is the unmanifest Brahman, free from modifications and the bestower of all accomplishments. You are his great power, known as Narayana, the embodiment of eternal bliss and the soul of all. Who is this Ganesha? Why do you, O Lord, remember him? Why does Shiva, the Lord of the Universe, worship him daily? O Keshava, please remove this doubt of mine. You are all-knowing; please instruct me, your disciple.

Mudgala said: Hearing Narada’s words, Keshava was pleased, seeing him as a true vessel for devotion to Ganesha.

Narayana said: Listen, Narada, do not doubt. There is no doubt that this Ganesha is the embodiment of Yoga and peace. He is the Lord of success and wisdom, residing in the city of Svananda. By the power of his devotion, we are the bestowers of all accomplishments. He is the unique leader (Vinayaka), and we, who are also leaders, worship him with devotion for the sake of peace and yoga. Once, I went to Kailasa and was astonished to see Shiva worshipping Ganesha. Seeing his complete daily ritual, I asked him: "You are the unmanifest Brahman, the Lord of Brahma, free from modification and illusion. Why do you, who are Sadashiva, worship the remover of obstacles? Who is this Ganesha? Please tell me."

Shiva said: Listen, O Vishnu, to this secret that grants peace through yoga. Ganesha is the Brahman that grants the state of oneness with the Divine. The five-fold movement of the mind is called wisdom (Buddhi). The supreme nature experiences everything through these five divisions. The accomplishment that grants illusion is the source of various delusions. For the sake of perfection, the mind strives in five ways. The attainment of righteousness, wealth, desire, and liberation is said to be the path to Brahman. Know, O Purushottama, that one who is established in Brahman has no such five-fold mind. Such a person is called a Yogi who has become one with Ganesha. The syllable 'Ga' represents the state of conscious meditation, and 'Na' represents the state of super-conscious (unmanifest) meditation. Ganesha is the master of both, always filled with peaceful yoga. I am the unmanifest "Not-this," and you are the embodiment of the Self. Wherever we are seen, we are all manifestations of existence. Ganesha is the one who became five-fold through the yoga of existence. In the Vedas, he is described as being full of Maya, but in reality, he is beyond Maya. He is attained only through peace and in no other way. I am Ganesha, the eternal Brahman, the Lord of all. This is the yoga of Ganesha that I have explained to you. By this method, the knowledge of Ganesha is attained. Abandoning the five-fold mind, become like the Chintamani, O Lord.

Having said this, Mahadeva fell silent. Narada, I have told you this knowledge of Ganesha. He is the one who grants us our positions and the state of Brahman. He is our family deity, and that is why we worship him.

Mudgala said: After bowing to Vishnu, the sage Narada went to a forest and engaged in the practice of yoga. Meditating on Ganesha in his mind and chanting the supreme mantra, after ten years, Ganesha was pleased and appeared before the sage. Seeing him, Narada was astonished and bowed down. With folded hands, the sage praised the Lord of the Ganas.

Narada said: I bow to the Lord of the Ganas, the destroyer of all obstacles, who is beyond the reach of Vedanta but is the Brahman itself. How can I praise the one who is beyond mind and speech, yet is the essence of both? O Heramba, by your vision, I am fulfilled. After worshipping Ganesha, Narada praised him again, his body trembling with emotion: Salutations to Ganesha, the Lord of obstacles, the destroyer of obstacles for his devotees and the creator of them for those who are not. Salutations to you, who are endowed with immeasurable Maya, the embodiment of Yoga, and the one who deludes the yogis. Salutations to you, Vinayaka, the Lord of all, the Chintamani. Salutations to the one with infinite glory, the one with the moon on his forehead. Salutations to the one-tusked God, the one who deludes the deluded. Salutations to the Supreme Lord, the highest of the high. Salutations to you, who are without qualities yet the source of all qualities, the witness. Salutations to the one who rides the mouse. Salutations to the beginningless one, the eldest, the seeker. You are the destroyer, the creator, the vessel, and the one who takes many forms. By drinking the nectar of your vision, my great delusion of death and duality has been destroyed. You have made me realize that I am Ganesha. I see nothing separate from you, O Lord of the Ganas. By your grace, I have attained the peace of yoga. Grant me supreme devotion to your lotus feet. Make me a devotee of yours, filled with love.

Having said this, he fell silent. Ganesha then said: My devotion will always be steady in you. You will never deviate from yoga, O great sage. You will be worshipped by the kings of yogis and will be respected by all. This hymn composed by you will grant the peace of yoga. Those who read or hear it will attain worldly enjoyment and liberation.

Having said this, the elephant-faced Lord merged into his heart. The sage himself began to see Ganesha in his heart forever. O Prajapati, this is the story of Narada that I have told you. Whoever hears or reads it will attain the supreme destination.

Chapter 4: The Slaying of Madhu and Kaitabha

Mudgala said: Then Brahma himself created the Brahmins from his mouth, the Kshatriyas from his arms, and the Vaishyas from his thighs. Driven by the gunas, the great Lord created the Shudras from his feet. Similarly, he created Sannyasa from his head, the Vanaprastha ashrama from his heart, Brahmacharya from his navel, and the householder life from his shanks. Seeing these Varnas and Ashramas, the Grandfather (Brahma) was filled with joy. Then Brahma himself created Dharma from his chest and Adharma from his back, which became the instigators of gods and demons respectively. From his own body, he created the gods, Gandharvas, Apsaras, Yakshas, demons, and spirits. He also created serpents, birds, animals, and humans, endowing them with action, and then he bestowed his own body upon them.

All these beings, endowed with great power, emerged from Brahma’s body, yet they did not multiply as expected. O Daksha, listen to the measure of their creation and the primary nature of the world as I explain it briefly. The first creation is said to be the Mahat-tattva of Brahma. The second is the creation of the subtle elements (Tanmatras). The third is the Vaikarika creation, known as the creation of the senses. These are the Prakrita (primary) creations born of intellect. The fourth is the Mukhya creation, consisting of stationary beings. The fifth is the Tiryaksrota, the animal kingdom. The sixth is the Urdhvasrota, the creation of the gods. The seventh is the Arvaksrota, the human creation. The eighth is the Bhautika creation, consisting of the elements. The ninth is the Kaumara creation, which the wise should know. Thus, six are known as Vaikrita (secondary) and three as Prakrita (primary), making nine types of creation in total. The three Prakrita creations are born of intellect, and all beings function according to these principles.

Having created these various beings, Brahma found that they did not multiply, which caused him great sorrow. Then, from his body, a divine couple emerged. From his left side came the beautiful Prakriti, known as Satarupa, and from his right side came the Purusha, known as Manu Svayambhuva, the noble and first knower of the Kshatriya dharma. With folded hands, they asked Brahma, "O noble Lord of creation, command us, what shall we do?" Brahma, filled with joy, said to them, "Create an incomparable progeny through the union of marriage, by the power of your penance." They bowed to him and went to a beautiful forest, where they performed severe penance, meditating on the Lord of the Ganas (Ganesha) in their hearts.

Then, from the shadow of the Creator, the great sage Kardama was born. Seeing him, Brahma said, "Create progeny." The sage went to the forest and performed supreme penance, meditating on Lord Vishnu in his white form, as prescribed, with the desire for progeny. Meanwhile, two extremely terrible demons named Madhu and Kaitabha were born from the earwax of Vishnu. By the strength of their arms, they conquered the three worlds, and all the gods, terrified, fled in all directions. When the demons went to devour Brahma, he praised the Goddess of Sleep (Nidra). Freed by her, Vishnu awoke and shone forth. A terrible battle ensued between them, but Janardana (Vishnu) could not defeat them in wrestling. For five thousand years, the imperishable Hari fought, but the powerful demons crushed him.

Then Vishnu, hiding himself, fled and went to Shankara (Shiva) with a heart full of devotion. Bowing to the great Lord, he said, "I am filled with fear. Tell me the story of this battle and the way to victory." Shiva said to him, "You have forgotten Ganesha. Worship him with effort, and you will attain success." Shiva gave him the six-syllabled mantra of Ganesha. Vishnu went to the Dandaka forest to perform penance. There, sitting in the lotus posture and meditating on the elephant-faced Lord, he performed supreme penance according to the six-syllabled mantra. After a thousand years of severe penance, Ganesha appeared before Vishnu to grant him a boon. Seeing him, Vishnu bowed and worshipped him with devotion. With a pleased heart, he praised the Lord of the Ganas.

Lord Vishnu said: Salutations to you, O Ganapati, Lord of Obstacles. Salutations to you, O treasure of mercy, Dhundhi, protector of your devotees. Salutations to you, Lord of Siddhi and Buddhi, remover of obstacles, who always creates obstacles for the non-devotees, O Heramba. Salutations to you, who are beyond qualities and the mover of qualities. Salutations to you, who are the form of the universe and yet devoid of it. Salutations to you, the giver of Brahman, the Brahman itself, the form of Brahman, the elephant-man, the one of many illusions. Salutations to you, the Lord of causes, the cause of all causes. Salutations to you, the King of the first, the one devoid of causes. Salutations to you, Ganesha, who resides in the hearts of yogis, the giver of yoga to yogis, the one who wears the form of yoga. Salutations to you, the refuge of Maya, the foundation of Maya. Salutations to you, the giver of delusion to the deluded. Who is capable of praising you, who exist in the form of yoga? Even the Vedas and all the scriptures with their limbs are baffled by you. O Lord, you are beyond mind and speech. How can I, who am not beyond mind and speech, praise you, O Lord of Obstacles? Yet, you are the giver of intellect, and by your grace, I have praised you. I am blessed, O Lord of Lords, by your vision today. Having seen you, Ganesha, I have become one with Brahman; there is no doubt that you are the King of Yogis and the giver of yoga.

Having said this, he bowed and danced, filled with devotion, his body trembling with joy and eyes shedding tears of happiness. The Lord of the Ganas said to him, "O Janardana, ask for a boon. I am pleased with your penance and devotion, and I will grant your desire. This hymn you have composed will be a giver of all success to those who read or hear it, O Vishnu. It will be a means to attain Dharma, Artha, Kama, and Moksha, and will grant desired objects immediately by my grace, O Keshava."

Mudgala said: Hearing the words of Ganesha, Vishnu was filled with joy and, bowing, asked for his desire. Vishnu said: "If you are pleased, then grant me firm and unwavering devotion to your lotus feet. Grant me, O Lord of the Ganas, incomparable knowledge, fame everywhere, and various powers. Tell me the way to destroy Madhu and Kaitabha, and grant me the strength to slay other demons, O Lord of Obstacles." Hearing his words, Ganesha said, "So be it. Go to battle, O Lord, and you will defeat those demons by your strength. Their death was kept very secret by Brahma at the time of granting the boon, as he was pleased by their penance. When those demons reveal the secret of their death by their own mouths, then slay those powerful demons in that manner. I, residing in your heart, will take refuge in Maya and make them subject to you; there is no doubt. When you remember me in your mind, O Vishnu, you will know the way to victory and the destruction of the demons."

Having said this, Ganesha, the Lord of Brahma, disappeared. Vishnu established a great image there with the help of Brahmins and worshipped it with abundant offerings. That place became a well-known Siddhi-Kshetra (place of success) from that time on. By performing rituals there, it became a famous Ganesha-Kshetra, served by the Siddhas. Vishnu always resided there with devotion, worshipping him daily with his family and attendants. Bowing to the Lord of the Ganas, he went to battle quickly. Meditating on Ganesha in his mind, he slew those two powerful demons.

Chapter 5 : The Granting of Boons to Svayambhuva

Salutations to Shri Ganesha.

Mudgala said: The nine sons of Brahma performed supreme penance. For a thousand divine years, they meditated upon Ganesha in their hearts. Constantly worshipping Him according to the single-syllable mantra and subsisting only on air, they pleased Him with great devotion. Then, Ganesha, who is fond of His devotees, became pleased with them and arrived to grant them boons, mounted upon His mouse. Seeing Him shining like infinite suns, they were filled with joy and bowed to His gentle form with folded hands. The sons of Daksha and others praised the Supreme Lord Ganesha, their necks bowed with devotion born from the realization of His vision.

The Prajapatis said: Salutations to You, the Lord of Obstacles; salutations to You, the witness of all. Salutations again and again to You, who are the soul of all and whose form is known only to Yourself. Salutations to You, the support of the worlds, the giver of knowledge, and the one who grants the path of yoga to the yogis. You are the creator, the sustainer, and the destroyer, assuming various forms. Salutations to You, who are both unmanifest and manifest, and the one who transcends both. Salutations to You, who are Brahma, Vishnu, Shankara, the Sun, Shakti, the Moon, and Yama. Salutations to You, who are Agni, Nirriti, Varuna, Vayu, Kubera, Rudra, and Shesha. Salutations to You, who are the form of planets and stars, the Siddhas and Sadhyas, the animals, humans, and trees. Salutations to You, who are the mountains, the oceans, the rivers, the demons, the lords of demons, and the rakshasas. Salutations to You, who are food, fruits, and flavors; You are the five elements and the form of all. Salutations to You, who are pure consciousness, knowledge, and the bodiless one. You are the untruth and the truth, the one who is equal in all and the fulfiller of desires. Salutations to You, O Ganesha, the giver of all peace, who are bliss itself, the non-yoga and the yoga, and the bearer of the Ganas. Who is capable of praising You, the illuminator of the mind and its functions? Therefore, we bow to You.

Mudgala said: Having praised Ganesha in this way, the Prajapatis bowed down. The Lord of the Ganas said to them, "Ask for various boons." This hymn composed by you shall be dear to Me. Whoever reads or hears it with devotion will be granted all attainments. It will grant them sons, grandsons, and spouses; it will remove the defects of barrenness and grant wealth, grain, and cattle. This hymn grants worldly enjoyment and liberation, leads to the state of Brahman, and fulfills all desires of men; there is no doubt in this. Hearing the words of Ganesha, they were filled with joy and, bowing with great devotion, spoke with folded hands.

They said: If You are pleased, O Ganesha, then grant us Your unwavering devotion, by which delusion is destroyed. Give us the unparalleled power of creation, O Lord of Obstacles. May whatever we desire always be fulfilled. Saying "So be it," the Lord of the Ganas disappeared. They too, having meditated upon Him in their minds, returned to their respective abodes.

Then Brahma spoke again to his excellent son Daksha, "You must perform penance for Shakti; she will become your daughter." Then, Daksha worshipped Shakti with devotion through penance. After a hundred years, she was pleased and came to him herself. He bowed to her and praised her with folded hands. Daksha said: Salutations to You, the form of Shakti, the form of illusion and delusion. Salutations to You, the mother of the world; salutations again and again to the mother of the world. Salutations to You, the giver of life to all, the one who takes all forms. Salutations to You, the beginningless one, the primordial being, the daughter of the mountain. Salutations to You, who are the form of Gayatri, Shri, and Savitri. Salutations to You, who are Prakriti and Purusha, the revealer of name and form, the attributeless one, and the holder of all attributes. Be pleased and become my daughter, O Dakshayani. Then Parvati said to her devotee, the Prajapati Daksha:

Shakti said: I will become your daughter, assuming various forms. I will fill the world after giving birth to children. This hymn of Mine, composed by you, will become famous. It will grant all desires to those who read or hear it. The Goddess disappeared while the Prajapati was watching. "You have abandoned the remembrance of Ganesha's name today. His mantra was abandoned by you due to your poor intellect. You insulted your daughter Sati, who then turned to ashes. You were also filled with sorrow by the Lord of Obstacles. Through the connection of past impressions, your memory has returned. Now, with a pure heart, listen to the nectar of His story. The wealth of a kingdom, when obtained, causes arrogance. Therefore, the wise do not desire the wealth that destroys knowledge."

The famous Manu Svayambhuva went to the forest with his wife. He pleased the Lord of Obstacles through the eight-syllable mantra. He performed terrible penance for a thousand divine years. Ganesha was pleased and came to grant him a boon. Seeing Him, Manu Svayambhuva bowed down first. Worshipping Him with supreme devotion, he praised the Lord of Obstacles.

Svayambhuva said: Salutations to the elephant-faced one, salutations to Heramba. Salutations to the one who is the form of Omkara and the one with attributes. Salutations to the one who is the elephant-faced in the attributeless state, the one who is always the bliss of Brahman. Salutations to Ganesha, who always nourishes His devotees. Salutations to the one who is in the form of a man and an elephant, the one who is the essence of yoga. Salutations to the four-armed one for the attainment of the goals of life. Salutations to the one who enjoys various pleasures and has the form of infinite play. Salutations to the Lord of Obstacles, the God who destroys the obstacles of His devotees. Salutations to the one who is the truth and the untruth, the one who is the unmanifest and the manifest. Salutations to You, the Lord of self-bliss, the constant giver of self-bliss. Salutations to You, who are mounted on a mouse and enjoy all things within. Salutations to You, who play in the ocean of various nectars. Salutations to You, the Lord of Siddhi and Buddhi, who spread the twofold illusion. Salutations to You, the giver of yoga to devotees and the form of yoga. Salutations to You, who are the great statement attainable through the Vedas and Upanishads. Salutations to You, who are the state of Brahman and the storehouse of Brahman. How can I praise You, O Lord of the Ganas, when even the Vedas are amazed, and the yogis, Shesha, and Shankara are bewildered? Yet, O Ganesha, by Your vision, inspiration has been attained, and thus I have praised You, O Lord. Oh, what good fortune, what good fortune, that the elephant-faced one has been seen. I am blessed on this earth, as You are beyond the reach of Vedanta. My birth, penance, knowledge, and lineage are blessed, O Lord. Everything, including my knowledge and vows, is blessed by the sight of Your feet.

Having praised the Lord of the Ganas with supreme devotion, he stood there with his hair standing on end and bowed again and again. Lifting him up, the Lord of the Ganas said to His excellent devotee, "Ask for the boon that is in your mind. This hymn of Mine, composed by you, will be pleasing and will grant everything to those who read or hear it. It will grant sons, grandsons, and prosperity, and will destroy sorrow. It will undoubtedly grant wealth, grain, and abundance. It will be the means for Dharma, Artha, Kama, and Moksha, and will grant the state of Brahman. It will undoubtedly increase firm devotion to Me." To the Lord of Obstacles speaking thus, the excellent Manu Svayambhuva, with a pleased heart and folded hands, said:

Manu said: If You are pleased, O Ganesha, then grant me firm devotion to You, by which there is no delusion. Give me the power to create, the best way to protect Dharma, and the ability to protect my subjects for a long time, O Lord of Gods. May whatever I desire, O Lord of Obstacles, always be fulfilled. May I always dwell among Your devotees, O giver of honor. Saying "So be it," the Lord of the Ganas disappeared and went to His own abode, for He is fond of His devotees.

Thus ends the fifth chapter named The Granting of Boons to Svayambhuva, in the second section of the Mudgala Purana.

Chapter 6 : Description of the Lineage of Daksha’s Daughters

Mudgala said: Thereafter, Svayambhuva Manu went and bowed before Brahma. Following his instructions, he and Shatarupa departed. Manu, the lord and wielder of weapons, ruled his kingdom righteously. Being virtuous and possessing great brilliance, he became a devotee of Ganesha. Through the path of procreation, Manu begot two sons and three daughters upon Shatarupa. His two wise sons were Priyavrata and Uttanapada, and his three beautiful daughters were Akuti, Devahuti, and Prasuti.

Manu gave Prasuti to you (Daksha), Akuti to Ruchi, and Devahuti to Kardama. Thus, the great king acted according to the divine ordinance. Kardama pleased Lord Vishnu through diligent penance. After a hundred years, the Lord was pleased and appeared before the sage to grant him a boon. Kardama requested, "May you be born as my son, O Lord of the Universe." Having granted the boon, Vishnu returned to Vaikuntha. Consequently, by the power of that boon, Vishnu took birth as the son of Devahuti in the form of Kapila.

From Akuti and Ruchi, a divine pair was born—Yajna and the goddess Dakshina—by whom the world was sustained. The glorious Lord Yajna begot twelve auspicious sons upon Dakshina, who are known as the Yamas. Thereafter, Lord Yajna, along with his wife, practiced penance and always pleased Ganesha through the path of yoga. He fashioned a four-armed image of Ganesha made of jewels and, accompanied by sages, worshipped Him daily with devotion. Pleased, Ganapati granted him supreme yoga, through which he became a master of the Ganapatya path. Yajna joyfully chanted the mantra "Heramba" and happily imparted the Ganapatya yoga to his disciples.

Daksha Prajapati begot twenty-four daughters upon Prasuti, who were all embodiments of Shakti and the mothers of the world. These thirteen—Shraddha, Lakshmi, Dhriti, Tushti, Pushti, Medha, Kriya, Buddhi, Lajja, Vapu, Shanti, Siddhi, and Kirti—Daksha, with a joyful heart and prompted by Brahma, gave in marriage to Dharma. I shall now tell you of their progeny.

Kama is known as the son of Shraddha, and Artha as the son of Lakshmi. Niyama is the son of Dhriti, and Santosha is the son of Tushti. Labha was born to Pushti, and Shama is known as the son of Medha. Danda and Samaya are the sons of Kriya. Bodha is the son of Buddhi, and Apramada is also attributed to her. Vinaya is the son of Lajja, and Vyavasaya is the son of Vapu. Kshema is the son of Shanti, Siddha is the son of Siddhi, and Yashas is the son of Kirti. These are the sons of Dharma. Harsha and Ananda were born to Kama. This is described as the auspicious creation of Dharma.

I shall now describe the creation of Adharma, which brings sorrow. From the back of Brahma, a pair was born: Adharma and Himsa. Their son is known as Anrita (Falsehood), and their daughter is Nikriti. From them, Bhaya (Fear) was born. Naraka (Hell) and Maya (Illusion) were born as their children. From Bhaya and Maya, Mrityu (Death), the destroyer of all, was born. From Naraka and Vedana, Duhkha (Sorrow), which causes worldly suffering, was born. From Mrityu, Vyadhi (Disease), Jara (Old Age), Shoka (Grief), Trishna (Thirst), and Krodha (Anger) were born. All these are known to be born of Adharma and lead to misery. They have no progeny, as they are all celibate.

Now, learn of the lineage of the remaining eleven daughters. Daksha gave Sati to Rudra, Khyati to Bhrigu, Sambhuti to Marichi, Smriti to Angira, Priti to Pulastya, Kshama to Pulaha, Santati to Kratu, Anasuya to Atri, Urja to Vashistha, Svaha to Agni, and the goddess Svadha to the Pitris.

From Sati and Shankara, the mighty Kartikeya was born, who was anointed by the Creator as the commander of the divine army. From Khyati and Bhrigu, Lakshmi, the beloved of Narayana, was born. The two gods, Dhatri and Vidhatri, are known as the sons-in-law of Meru. Ayati and Niyati were the daughters of Meru. From them, Dhatri and Vidhatri begot two excellent sons. Mrikanda and Prana were born; Markandeya was the son of Mrikanda, and the brilliant Vedashira was the son of Prana.

Marichi, through Sambhuti, begot Purnamasa and four daughters endowed with all auspicious marks: Krishi, Vrishti, Krishti, and the fourth, Apachiti. Viraja and Parvata were the sons of Purnamasa. Kshama bore sons to Pulaha Prajapati: Karma-shreshtha, Variyan, Sahishnu, and the great sage Vanakavi, who was a son of great penance and brilliance.

I shall now describe the lineage of Atri, O Shaunaka. Listen with devotion. Atri begot auspicious sons upon Anasuya: Soma, Durvasa, and Dattatreya, the master of yoga. These three are known as the sons of the great Atri, being partial incarnations of Brahma, Shiva, and Vishnu. From Smriti and Angira, the beautiful Siniwali was born, along with Kuhu, Raka, and the chaste Anumati. From Priti and Pulastya, the great yogi Agastya was born, who is known to be the same sage from his previous birth. Devabahu was also a daughter, known for her perfect beauty. From Santati and Kratu, sixty thousand sons were born, known as the Valakhilyas, who were all celibate.

Vashistha begot seven sons upon Urja: Chitraketu, Surochaka, Viraja, Mitra, Ulbana, Vasubhrit, and Dyuman, along with a lotus-eyed daughter. Shakti and others were also sons of great brilliance. From Svaha and the fire-god (who is a form of Rudra), three sons were born: Pavaka, Pavamana, and Shuchi. They in turn begot sons, making a total of forty-nine fires. All are brilliant and receive shares in sacrifices. Pavamana is associated with the fire produced by friction, and Pavaka is associated with lightning. Shuchi is the fire that burns in the sun. This fire, which pervades everything and is a form of Rudra, is called the son of Brahma.

The Pitris, known as Agnishvattas and Barhishadas, had two daughters, Mena and Dharani. Mena bore Mainaka and Krauncha to Himavan, as well as the world-purifying Ganga and the supreme goddess Parvati. Both attained Lord Shankara as their husband. Through the penance of the mountain king and Mena, the goddess Sati was pleased and, by the power of the boon, was born as their daughter, Parvati. Dharani, the wife of Mount Meru, bore the daughters Ayati and Niyati, as mentioned before.

I have thus described the lineage of Daksha’s daughters. Whoever reads or hears this attains the fruits of all their desires.

Chapter 7 : Description of Enjoyment and Liberation

Salutations to Lord Ganesha.

Mudgala said: The ancestors, born from the daughters of Daksha, were held in high esteem. Out of respect, they each performed the worship of Ganesha. After performing various mantras according to the prescribed rites in the forest, they meditated upon Heramba and remained absorbed in penance. Pleased after a thousand years, the elephant-faced Lord, satisfied by their devotion, went there to grant them boons. Seeing the Lord, all of them, with folded hands and filled with devotion, praised Ganesha, the bestower of all accomplishments.

The sons of the sages said: Salutations to you, O Lord with the curved trunk, the protector of devotees. Salutations to you, who are the embodiment of Brahman and who have attained the state of Brahman. Salutations to you, who are the form of mind, speech, and movement, and whose form is attainable through the mind. Salutations to you, who are the form of Yoga, the Yoga itself, and the Yogi. Salutations to you, O formless remover of obstacles, who are the embodiment of peace and the bestower of peace everywhere. Salutations to you, the creator, protector, and destroyer of the universe. Salutations to you, the one worthy of worship by all, the source of all, and the primordial form. Salutations to you, who are devoid of the distortions of Maya, the wielder of Maya, the giver of delusion, the support of Maya, and the controller of Maya. Salutations to you, the witness of all, who possess infinite power, infinite glory, and infinite faces and hands.

O Ganesha, you are the one beyond the reach of the Vedas, yet you have been seen by us due to the greatness of our fortune. We are incapable of praising you. O Lord, our father is blessed, and we are the most blessed. Our knowledge, vows, penance, and mantras have become fruitful by your vision. If you are pleased, O One-Tusked Lord, grant us firm devotion to you, so that the delusion caused by Maya may be destroyed. O elephant-faced Lord, grant us the ability to perform our respective duties, and may all our desires be fulfilled. Having said this, they worshipped him with various offerings, bowed down with devotion, and were filled with joy.

The Lord then said to them: Whatever you desire will be fulfilled without a doubt. Devotion to my feet will be blissful, and you will attain success in your respective tasks and gain firm fame. This hymn composed by you will grant all accomplishments. Whoever listens to it or recites it with devotion will attain success. Whoever recites it twenty-one times for twenty-one days will immediately attain the fruit of their heart's desire without doubt. Having said this, the Lord of the Ganas disappeared. They all returned to their homes filled with joy. All the men and women born of Daksha's daughters became happy and wise in their respective duties.

Shaunaka said: O Suta, tell me in detail about the highly fortunate sons of Atri, who are embodiments of Brahma, Vishnu, and Shiva.

Suta said: The Moon, born from a portion of Brahma, performed penance. Having obtained a boon from Ganesha, he became nectar-like. He became the nourisher of all gods and men through his phases, and through his rays, he always increased the growth of food. Dattatreya, born from a portion of Vishnu, became a practitioner of Yoga. Through the path of Yoga, he gradually attained various states of Brahman. O sage, he manifested various states such as that of a dullard, a madman, a ghost, or a child, revealing different levels of existence. Seeing him as a Yogi, all people, including Brahmins and those of other castes and ashramas, became devoted to his service.

The great sage Dattatreya could not find a moment of rest. Distressed, he thought, "What should I do?" Then, through the power of his Yoga, he submerged himself in water. All the people stood on the bank waiting for him. Then, Dattatreya emerged from the water holding the Goddess of Liberation (Moksha-Lakshmi) in his left hand. In his right hand, he held a pot of wine, which he drank himself. Along with the woman, he lived in enjoyment and luxury. Seeing this, the followers of Atri thought he had fallen from his path, but the son of Atri remained established in his Yoga with ease.

After some time, a fierce war broke out between the gods and demons nearby. The gods were defeated by the demons and fled in all directions. They came to Dattatreya, terrified, and cried, "Protect us, protect us!" The demons, seeing the beautiful Goddess of Liberation (Moksha-Lakshmi), seized her and placed her on their heads out of delusion. If they had held her at their feet, they would have undoubtedly attained victory. Instead, touched by the Goddess of Liberation on their heads, the demons were drained of their power, oppressed by the gods, and fled in fear back to the netherworld. Then, right before their eyes, the Goddess of Liberation disappeared into the Brahman.

Shaunaka said: O Suta, what you have said is puzzling. How can the Goddess of Liberation, when touched by the heads of the demons, cause them suffering?

Suta said: You have asked well, O Brahmin. Listen carefully to what I heard from the all-knowing Vyasa. The demons, filled with passion and darkness, were devoted to the Goddess of Enjoyment (Bhoga-Lakshmi). They were thieves, adulterers, and oppressors of others. They were always addicted to intoxicants and sinful acts. They were critics of the virtuous, sages, gods, and scriptures. Because of these many faults, these great demons were always fond of enjoyment and never worthy of liberation. Having attained the body, they abandoned liberation and set their minds on enjoyment. Therefore, they struck Liberation with their feet, while they worshipped the Goddess of Enjoyment on their heads.

The gods and sages always strive for liberation, so they hold the Goddess of Liberation on their heads. When the demons held Liberation on their heads, it did not bring them auspiciousness because they were filled with evil. Since they were addicted to sense objects and did not desire Liberation, the Goddess was, in effect, struck by their feet. Therefore, those who desire enjoyment should worship the Goddess of Enjoyment. If those with evil conduct hold onto Liberation, it will destroy them. This is the reason, O sage. Conversely, those who desire liberation treat the Goddess of Enjoyment as if she were struck by their feet. Where enjoyment is loved, liberation does not exist; where liberation is loved, how can there be attachment to enjoyment? Know, O Shaunaka, that the Goddess of Enjoyment is associated with desire, while the Goddess of Liberation is free from desire.

Chapter 8 : Dattacarita

Salutations to Shri Ganesha.

Mudgala said: Thus, practicing the path of Yoga through the association of Dattatreya, the expert Avadhuta shone forth, endowed with various behaviors. At times, like a cow or a bird, this best of sages performed bodily functions such as urination in public. Being in a natural state, the son of Atri eventually became troubled, realizing his own inner state of being. Though established in the various states of Brahman—manifest and unmanifest—he did not find peace through Yoga, so he went to his father.

Bowing down to the divine Atri, who was surrounded by yogis, Dattatreya stood before him with folded hands, seeing him as the embodiment of the complete Brahman. Honored by Atri, he sat on a seat and spoke to his father, the son of Brahma, with great humility.

Datta said: O Master, I have attained various types of Brahman through Yoga. I have perceived the adjuncts (upadhis) and have also become free from them. Having abandoned both the adjunct and the non-adjunct, I became established in equanimity. Leaving that behind, I became the Turiya (the fourth state) and remained in my natural state. O great yogi, O Lord, seeing my own independence there, I have become devoid of peace. Tell me, what should I do? Beyond the natural state, no higher Brahman is attained through the service of Yoga. Therefore, O great sage, tell me the Yoga of peace.

Hearing the words of Datta, the son of Brahma, the great ascetic Atri, spoke with respect to his son who was seeking peace. Atri said: Listen, son, I will tell you the eternal Yoga of peace, which was told to me by Brahma, through which I attained peace instantly. You have attained the Lord Ganapati as our family deity; he is declared to be the family deity of Shiva and other gods. Know this, O wise one, as the form of the Yoga of peace. Worship him with great effort, and you will attain peace.

Everything originated from Ganesha, and by him, it is sustained, O son. By his worship alone, Shiva and others have accomplished their tasks. The entire world, consisting of name and form, is called Brahman. That which is known as the power (Shakti) is the supreme, true form of Brahman. Know that which is nectar-like, solar, and established in the form of the Self to be the true Brahman described in the Vedas. Knowing the non-difference between these two, understand that Brahman is established everywhere. Know Vishnu, through the Vedas, as being both existent and non-existent.

That which is distinct from these, the Turiya, is described as the "Not this" (Neti) form. The wise know this as the independent Brahman, known as the passionless Shiva. The bliss of the Self in the union of the four Brahmans is described by the wise. That, according to the knowers of the Vedas, is Ganesha, who is directly the form of Maya. All internal and external actions appear as the form of Brahman; that is known as Karma Yoga, the union of actions, O son. The Yoga that is the non-dualist realization of the yogis, which arises through the eye of knowledge, is called Jnana Yoga. The Yoga of equanimity, which is the non-difference between knowledge and action, is the blissful form attained upon the destruction of duality.

The Mahatma who holds Karma Yoga by his own will, and likewise Jnana Yoga and Ananda Yoga by his own will, and who is devoid of these three by his own will, is called the "Natural" (Sahaja). He is always independent and plays by his own will. The Yoga that is the form of Brahman is called the Self-Bliss (Svananda). Where there is independence, how can there be dependence? For one who has become Brahman, there is no rising or falling from the Self-form due to any union. In the Self-Bliss, the union of the worlds and Brahmans occurs; this Yoga is non-dual, and therefore it is associated with Maya. The Yoga that is non-union is always devoid of union; therefore, there is no entry of the worlds and Brahmans into it.

The Yoga that is always devoid of Maya and beyond self-perception is held by yogis as the cessation of the self due to the absence of difference. That which is established in Brahman, which neither comes nor goes, is the non-union state of Brahmanhood, attained through the destruction of Self-Bliss. He who dwells in the Brahman-Self-Bliss is called Ganesha by the wise. He is not described by the knowers of the Vedas as being merely the Self-Bliss. Ganesha, the Lord of play, is described as the Self-Bliss, remaining in his own form through the state of union. Ganesha, devoid of play, is described as the form of Yoga; being devoid of bliss, he is always established in Brahman.

Upon the destruction of both union and non-union, the Lord of Ganas is described by yogis, through the service of Yoga, as the form of the Yoga of peace. Ganesha is described as the form of complete Yoga; the state of being devoid of Maya is described as mere illusion. Know the intellect, O son, to be of the form of the five-fold mind. There, the delusion-causing power is the illusion-giving, delusion-form. The attainment of Dharma, Artha, Kama, and Moksha appears different. The attainment that is the form of Brahman is described by yogis in that way. Whatever is reflected in the five-fold mental modification, O son, that is the supreme reflected form of the Lord of Ganas. The power that is remembered in Dharma, Artha, Kama, and Moksha, which are Brahman-like, is the wealth that causes delusion, in which the creature wanders in desire. Know that the reflection in the five-fold wealth is that of Ganapa, O son, through the service of the Yoga of peace. Upon the destruction of the five-fold mind and the dissolution of the five-fold wealth, you will undoubtedly become the Lord of Ganas.

Abandon the Avadhuta path and become the chief Avadhuta, respectfully shaking off the great mind and great wealth. Therefore, quickly worship peace through the Yoga of Datta. I will give you the great mantra of Ganesha according to the prescribed rules.

Then, the son of Brahma, the best of those who know Yoga, gave the one-syllable mantra to his son with devotion, and after bowing to him, departed. Datta, the best of those who know Yoga, who was the direct form of Vishnu, worshipped the Lord of Obstacles on the southern bank of the Ganges. Abandoning the worldly form and establishing himself in peace, the knower of Yoga worshipped Ganapa with a pure heart, meditating on him.

O Shaunaka, the great yogi Datta, within one year, attained peace and became a devotee of Ganesha. To see his devotee, Ganesha went there by his own will, out of affection for his devotee, to the ashram that bestows happiness and peace. Seeing him, Datta suddenly rose, bowed with folded hands, and praised the Lord of Obstacles, his family deity, becoming steady.

Shri Datta said: Salutations to you, O Ganapati, salutations to the form of Yoga. Salutations to the giver of Yoga to yogis, and salutations to the form of the Yoga of peace. Salutations to you, the Lord of Siddhi and Buddhi, the holder of the five-fold mind. Salutations again and again to Ganesha, who delights in various pastimes. Salutations to you, the giver of Siddhi, the bestower of various riches. Salutations again and again to Heramba, the destroyer of delusion and the creator of delusion. Salutations to you, who dwells in Self-Bliss, who holds the non-difference of union. Salutations again and again to the Lord of Obstacles, who plays with various illusions. Salutations to you, the Sankhya, the one established in Brahman, the wise one who is devoid of ignorance. Salutations to you, the form of rising from the Supreme, the bodiless one. Salutations to you, the knowledge, the form of all, the one who is the body and the embodied. Salutations to you, who rises from the Self, the dissolution of nature. Salutations to you, the God with ego, the Lord of the world. Salutations to you, the great one, the form of the point (Bindu), the form of the world. Salutations to you, the Lord of Gunas who is the form of sound (Nada), the wearer of various forms. Salutations to you, O grandfather, the Brahman, the creator of creation. Salutations to you, O Hari, the protector, the wearer of various bodies. Salutations to you, O Shankara, the destroyer, the form of action, the sun. Salutations to you, O Dhundhiraja, the power, the form of action, the form of god and man, the form of serpents and demons. Salutations to you, the immovable, salutations again and again to the movable. Salutations to you, the God who is the form of the world, the Brahman. How can I praise you, O Lord of Ganas, who are established in the form of Yoga? The Vedas and others are not capable; therefore, I bow to you. My body, my parents, my lineage, my knowledge, my Yoga, and my penance are blessed today by the sight of your lotus feet.

Having said this, the great sage, overwhelmed with devotion, danced with hair standing on end, shedding tears of joy repeatedly. Then, holding him by his hand, the Lord of Ganas embraced him and spoke wonderful words to the perfect yogi.

Shri Ganesha said: You are blessed, O Datta, the best of yogis; you have been born by my grace. You will always attain complete and unshakable peace in me. O great sage, there will never be any difference in you. To increase your love, I will remain here motionless. You have heard the greatness of the knowledge of Ganesha from the mouth of Atri. Therefore, this is a field of science (Vijnana) that brings direct realization. I shall be known as Vijnana-Ganapa, named after Datta. O giver of honor, I will be the bestower of peace here upon being seen. Those who dwell here for my sake with devotion, I will always give them the Yoga of peace in your presence. Long ago, Lord Shiva himself attained the supreme knowledge here. I named that Shankara as Vijnaneshwara. Having stayed in his presence here, you have attained knowledge. Therefore, by my grace, you have attained friendship with Shankara. This hymn composed by you will be the bestower of Yoga and peace. For those who read or hear it, it will lead to Brahmanhood. It will be the bestower of Dharma, Artha, Kama, and Moksha. It will grant all Siddhis and bestow knowledge upon all.

Having said this, Ganesha, who loves his devotees, disappeared. Datta, feeling distressed, meditated on him and remained there, O Lord of the people. Then, by his grace, he surrendered his own self there, and having become non-different through Yoga, he became one who has surrendered his self. The distinction that "Datta is the devotee and Ganesha is his master" was destroyed. He remained in that form, as the servant in the master. This devotion to Ganesha, which is the form of self-surrender, was accomplished by Datta through the effort of the Yoga of peace.

In the meantime, Lord Shiva himself appeared there. He, known as Vijnaneshwara, embraced Datta. O Datta, O great one, you have become my friend. Let us establish this Lord of Ganas here through the service of Yoga. Then, placing Datta in front, Shankara, along with the Brahmins, established Ganesha on the southern bank of the Ganges. The sages gave him the name Vijnana-Ganaraja. From that time, that place became famous as the field of Ganapa. This holy field, named Vijnana, bestows the fruit of the Yoga of peace. It is a field that grants all Siddhis to those who travel there. The Lord of Ganas was established by them on the fourth day of the bright fortnight of Jyeshtha. Therefore, people perform great festivities there on that day. This excellent field is served by yogis, gods, Gandharvas, Siddhas, Nagas, and humans, as well as various other people. Whoever hears or reads this greatness of Datta, there is no doubt that he will attain all Siddhis.

Thus ends the eighth chapter named Dattacarita in the second section of the Mudgala Purana.

Chapter 9 : The Description of Priyavrata Attaining the Kingdom

Salutations to Shri Ganesha.

Suta said: Durvasa, an expansion of Shankara, performed supreme penance. Having attained the state of Brahman, the great sage became one with the Absolute. Seeing him full of anger, restless, and longing for peace, the great sage Atri bestowed knowledge upon him, just as Dattatreya had received. In the region of Mayuresha, he engaged in the meditation of Ganesha, and through his devotion, the Lord of the Ganas granted him the state of peace. From that time on, the great yogi Durvasa became a possessor of peace, free from anger, and a knower of Brahman. I have narrated to you the history of the sons of Atri, just as it was revealed by Mudgala to Daksha, O learned one. Now, I shall narrate the history of the lineage of Svayambhuva Manu, which destroys all sins, as told by Mudgala.

Shaunaka asked: How did the wise sage Mudgala narrate that history to him? Tell me everything, just as Daksha heard it.

Suta said: Daksha, deluded by a curse, heard the history of the sons of Atri and the nature of yoga, and spoke again to Mudgala.

Daksha said: Blessed is Atri, whose sons are powerful devotees of Ganesha, yogis, and bestowers of yoga. I have heard their history. Now, O Brahman, tell me about the ancient path of creation and what the two powerful sons of Svayambhuva Manu did.

Suta said: Hearing the words of Daksha, the great yogi Mudgala, who knew the supreme truth, spoke with a joyful heart.

Mudgala said: Svayambhuva Manu had two sons, both perfectly righteous, known as Priyavrata and Uttanapada, who were famous as Prajapatis. The elder, Priyavrata, went to the forest, intent on spiritual practice. Thinking that the body and all worldly things are perishable, Manu became distressed. Narada suddenly arrived there, and Manu said to him, "O learned one, please instruct my son Priyavrata according to the scriptures." Saying "So be it," Narada went to his great hermitage. Seeing him, the royal sage rose and bowed with folded hands. Seeing his son standing there, bound by his own duty, the glorious Narada spoke to the best of kings.

Narada said: Listen, O noble and wise Priyavrata. You have realized that the body and everything else is perishable, which is a great wonder. Nevertheless, this human body is burdened by three debts. The entire world is rooted in the actions performed by the human body. There is no doubt that this human body is the form of food for all; therefore, one who possesses a body must nourish it. By following one's own duty according to the Varna and Ashrama system, and by performing righteous deeds, the world is nourished. Through sacrifices, one repays the debt to the gods; through Shraddha and other rites, the debt to the ancestors; and through the path of procreation, one fulfills the debt to humanity. Why have you come to the forest while still burdened by these debts? Rule the kingdom, O noble one, with joy and devotion to your duty. After establishing your sons in the eternal kingdom, you may then enter the forest and engage in the practice of yoga.

Mudgala said: Hearing the words of Narada, the son of Manu, the best of kings, was delighted and spoke to him with folded hands and devotion.

Priyavrata said: O Master, I wish to ask you, O wise one, by what means can a human being become one with Brahman?

Narada said: Listen, O son, I will tell you the path that leads to the state of Brahman, by which a human being attains peace. By knowing Ganesha, the Lord of Brahman, as the embodiment of yoga, a creature becomes one with Brahman through practice. The word "Gana" is said to denote a group; groups are of the nature of yoga, divided into external and internal. O son, the collection of all food is called "Annamaya" Brahman in the Vedas; thus, various Ganas are mentioned. Knowing Him as the Lord of those Ganas, the yogis of old attained peace, specifically the supreme state of peace through yoga. This Gajanan (Ganesha) is our family deity; worship Him with effort, and then there will be well-being. By worshipping the Lord of Ganas with devotion, our grandfather became one with Brahman. His sons are known as Brahma, Vishnu, Maheshwara, Surya, and Shakti; these five, being enjoyers, worshipped Him. O son, they attained supreme peace, and we, the sons of Brahma, worship that Lord of Ganas. Thus, we have become the foremost of yogis, O Priyavrata, endowed with all knowledge and free from bondage. Likewise, you too, O King, worship Ganesha with devotion. Everything will become easily accessible to you, O sinless one.

Then the great sage gave him the single-syllable mantra and taught him the entire process of worshipping Ganesha with effort. In the meantime, the wise Svayambhuva Manu arrived with Brahma; seeing them, the two sons bowed down. Instructed by Brahma, the grandson Priyavrata, along with Manu, returned to his city. Svayambhuva Manu established his sons in the kingdom and went to the forest, where he worshipped Ganesha with devotion through yoga. Gradually, he transcended the five planes of the mind, abandoned the mind, and became one with Brahman. Embracing the yoga of peace, he went to Brahmaloka. Remaining there, he stayed absorbed in the worship of Ganesha. Priyavrata himself ruled the earth, earning fame through righteousness, justice, and charity. All the people, engaged in their respective duties and following the Varna and Ashrama system, were happy and truthful under the reign of Priyavrata.

Chapter 10 : Description of the Islands

Salutations to Lord Ganesha.

Mudgala said: King Priyavrata had ten highly powerful, brilliant, righteous, and renowned sons. Agnidhra, Idhmajihva, the great Yajnabahu, Vira, Hiranyareta, Savana, Ghrtaprstha, Medhatithi, Vitihotra, and Kavi were all equal to their forefathers. Among them, Kavi, Savana, and Vira did not desire to rule. Therefore, the king appointed his other seven sons as rulers of the seven islands. He made his son Agnidhra the king of Jambudvipa. He appointed Idhmajihva as the ruler of Plaksadvipa and anointed Yajnabahu as the king of Shalmalidvipa. He made Hiranyareta the ruler of Kushadvipa and Ghrtaprstha the ruler of Kraunchadvipa. He established Medhatithi as the eternal king of Shakadvipa and appointed Vitihotra as the ruler of Pushkaradvipa.

Thereafter, the powerful King Priyavrata retired to the forest. By the grace of Narada, he practiced yoga and attained the status of a Gana (attendant of Ganesha). By the end of his life, through the practice of yoga, he attained the form of Ganesha. There has never been, nor will there ever be, a king like Priyavrata.

In Pushkaradvipa, Vitihotra had two sons, Ramana and Dhataki, who were both highly valiant and righteous. The region named after the great soul Ramana is known as Ramanaka, and the region named after Dhataki is known by scholars as Dhatakikhanda. In the center of this island lies the circular mountain known as Manasa, which is ten thousand yojanas in height. The lifespan of the inhabitants there is ten thousand years. Lord Brahma himself resides in this island of Pushkara. Gods and humans worship him there constantly. O Daksha, this island is surrounded on all sides by an ocean of sweet water, which is sixty-four lakh yojanas wide. At the end of this ocean lies the beautiful mountain known as Lokaloka. On one side of this mountain, there is habitation, and on the other, it is devoid of life. The light of the sun and moon reaches only up to the Lokaloka mountain. Beyond that, O Daksha, there is darkness on all sides; hence, sages have named it Lokaloka.

In Pushkara, there are many rivers originating from the Manasa mountain, which are filled with sweet, holy, and pure water. By drinking this water, painful diseases are destroyed, and fatigue or perspiration never occurs.

The ruler of Shakadvipa had seven sons: Pavamana, Purojava, Manojava, Dhumranika, Chitrarepha, Bahurupa, and Jagaddhara. There are seven main mountains and seven regions in that island. The first region is named after Purojava, and another is named after Manojava. The third is Pavamanaka, the fourth is Dhumranika, the fifth is Chaitrarephaka, the sixth is Bahurupa, and the seventh is Vaishvadhara. They ruled their respective regions peacefully. The lifespan of the people there is ten thousand years, and they remain eternally youthful and free from defects. This excellent island is surrounded by an ocean of curd, which is thirty-two lakh yojanas in width. The people of this island worship Lord Vayu.

The extent of Kraunchadvipa is sixteen lakh yojanas. There are seven regions, seven mountains, and seven rivers there, along with many smaller rivers. The seven sons of Ghrtaprstha ruled these seven regions, which were named after them: Amomadhuruha, Ghrtaprstha, Sudhatmaka, Bhrajistha, Lohita, Varnavanaspati, and the seventh. The lifespan of the people there is eleven thousand years, and they worship Lord Narayana in his water-borne form. This island is surrounded by an ocean of milk and is known as Shvetadvipa.

The seven sons of Hiranyareta, the king of Kushadvipa, were Vasu, Vasudana, Drdharuci, Satyavrata, Nabhigupta, Vivikta, and Devaka. The seven regions were named after them. There are seven boundary mountains and seven swift, holy rivers there. The lifespan of the people is ten thousand years, and the island is eight lakh yojanas in extent. It is surrounded by an ocean of ghee. The people there worship Lord Agni.

The sons of the king of Shalmalidvipa, Yajnabahu, were Surochana, Saumanasya, Ramana, Devavarshaka, Apyayana, Paribhadra, and the seventh, Vijnata. The seven regions were named after them. There are seven rivers and seven mountains there, and the island is four lakh yojanas in extent. It is surrounded by an ocean of wine. The lifespan of the people there is eight thousand years.

The sons of the king of Plaksadvipa, Idhmajihva, were Shiva, Yavayasa, Kshantakshema, Subhadraka, Amrta, and Abhaya. The seven regions were named after them. There are seven mountains and seven rivers there. The lifespan of the people is five thousand years, and the island is two lakh yojanas in extent. It is surrounded by an ocean of sugarcane juice.

In the islands starting from Plaksha up to Shaka, the people are devoted to their own duties according to their varna and ashrama. In Pushkara, all are knowledgeable in dharma, follow a single path, and belong to one varna and ashrama. There is no division of yugas there; the time remains constant, and the dharma is eternal. There is no greed or jealousy; the people are righteous, satisfied with their own spouses, and enjoy the fruits of their actions. They live like gods, constantly playing and enjoying. By worshipping the Lord residing in their respective islands, they attain happiness and eventually reach the four stages of liberation, such as Salokya.

Chapter 11 : Description of Geography

Salutations to Shri Ganesha.

Mudgala said: In Jambudvipa, the Lord had nine sons: Nabhi, Kimpurusha, Hari, Ilavrita, Hiranmaya, Kuru, Ramya, Bhadrasva, and Ketumala. The father himself gave them regions marked by their own names. After bestowing these seven regions upon his sons, they went to the forest, performed penance, and attained the state of Ganesha. They reached the abode of the Lord of Ganas, from where there is no return, due to the power of their yoga and devotion.

The expanse of Jambudvipa is one hundred thousand yojanas in all directions, surrounded by the salt ocean. In its center stands the great mountain Meru, which is golden, auspicious, adorned with various minerals, and rich in medicinal herbs. This golden mountain is filled with rivers of pure water, caves, and various creatures. The height of this great mountain is said to be one hundred thousand yojanas, with sixty thousand yojanas as its measurement. Its outer width is thirty-two thousand yojanas, and its base is eight thousand yojanas all around. This golden mountain stands divided between the lower and upper realms, shining in the middle of the earth in the shape of a lotus.

Surrounding it are mountains shaped like lotus petals, adorned with various minerals. In these places, there are wonderful dwellings for gods, Guhyakas, birds, yogis, demons, Siddhas, great souls, serpent kings, sages, Gandharvas, Prajapatis, ghosts, and spirits. There are marvelous gardens and playgrounds for the gods, and the waters everywhere dispel old age and sorrow. Eight great mountains, including Kailasa, are situated in the directions, where Rudra and other lords of the directions reside. In the center is the great forest, the abode of Brahma, the supreme progenitor. Thus, the mountain shines, providing happiness to all the gods.

To its south are three regions, and to the north, three regions are described. Two regions are situated in the east and west, and one region is in the center. Thus, nine regions are described in the middle of the island, with the Himalayas and other mountains serving as their boundaries. These great mountains are two thousand yojanas in height and eight thousand yojanas in width all around. Each region has a width of one thousand yojanas, where people live in their own distinct ways. In these regions, there are mountains and various rivers, and the people there live in happiness.

Except for Ajanabha (Bharatvarsha), the other eight regions are described as places where natural perfection is permanently established. The men and women there are always youthful, beautiful, enjoy happiness like the gods, and are endowed with virtuous qualities. They are attached to one another, enjoy pleasures simultaneously, and are devoted to their duties, righteous, and compassionate. There is no concept of Yuga-dharma (time-bound cycles) there; they always follow common righteousness. O respected one, there is no suffering caused by heat or cold in those regions. People there live for eleven hundred, twelve hundred, thirteen hundred, or sometimes five hundred or ten hundred years.

The region of Nabhi, known as Ajanabha, is near the Himalayas. It is known as the land of action (Karma-bhumi) and is respected by all. Having performed actions there, people go to various islands, regions, and the underworld. They go to heaven to enjoy the fruits of their actions, and after enjoying them, they return here to perform further actions. Or, having performed sinful deeds, they go to hell and then return here again. There is no doubt that by practicing yoga here, one can become a master yogi and attain the form of Ganesha through devotion.

Therefore, this region of the Himalayas is said to be the most excellent. It is the giver of all things and is respected by all. Wise men say that compared to the pleasures of other regions, this one is small, as it is subject to heat, cold, and various diseases. There are four castes here, and due to their deviations, various castes with different duties are described. In this land, the measurements of time and the laws of the ages apply. People are diverse in nature and driven by self-interest.

There are many mountains like the Vindhya and Sahyadri ranges. Many rivers originate from them and flow everywhere. They are holy and grant merit upon bathing, with sweet waters. People here are performers of action, endowed with three types of conduct. There are holy places for all the gods and the twice-born, which grant fruits according to the actions of those who serve them. Because of these qualities, this region is known as Bharatvarsha, as it is the land where actions are sustained.

This entire earth has been described to you briefly. Hearing or reading this removes all sins.

Thus ends the eleventh chapter of the second section of the Mudgala Purana, titled "The Description of Geography."

Chapter 12 : Description of the Solar Sphere

Salutations to Shri Ganesha.

Mudgala said: I shall now describe the supreme arrangement of all the worlds, and I will tell you briefly about the principal heavens. The Bhurloka (Earth), Bhuvarloka, Svarloka, Maharloka, Janaloka, Tapoloka, and Satyaloka are known as the worlds born from the cosmic egg. The Bhurloka is described in the Puranas, O Prajapati, as extending as far as the rays of Aryama (the Sun) and the Moon illuminate. It is accepted by the scriptures that this Bhurloka, which consists of seven islands, is accessible to humans by foot. The measurement that exists for the entire Bhurloka all around is said to be the same measurement for the Bhuvarloka. The Bhuvarloka is declared to extend from the Bhurloka up to the Sun, and the Svarloka is said by the wise to extend from there up to the Pole Star (Dhruva).

In that space, eight types of winds are described, O Lord: Avaha, Pravaha, Anvaha, Samvaha, Vivaha, Paravaha, Parivaha, and the one known as Vaha above them. The sphere of the Sun is situated at a distance of one hundred thousand yojanas from the Earth. The sphere of the Moon is said to be situated one hundred thousand yojanas from the Sun. The entire constellation of stars shines one hundred thousand yojanas from there. Two hundred thousand yojanas from there is the sphere of the planets.
